Every readout that moved did so at depth or at the response site: late-site TPR@1%FPR -0.144 vs prompt-site +0.0006, and parent-child |cos| 0.992 shallow vs 0.459 deep.\", \"EXPLICIT SCREEN over 14 named single-model candidates (C1-C14), all computable from harvests on disk or one prompt-only forward, with a selection rule fixed before scoring: beat BL1 by rho >= 0.15 with a CI excluding zero, a nonzero partial rho given BL1, escape the shuffled-label band, stay silent on the null-edit pair and random-init arm, and stay off ceiling.\", \"HELD-OUT CONFIRMATION separated: the 2 sealed families, the never-loaded 54-scenario XSTest split and a fresh graded set of at least 6 held-out checkpoints, used once with no re-screening.\", \"BARS RAISED: BL1, B3, B7 and the Jorak scanner'\n\nevidence_state => \"weak_or_null\"\n\nmove => \"widen\"\n\nmove_rationale => \"Screen ran to completion: survivor NONE, and the only readout that separated children from parents tracks the logit baseline. 3 iterations remain, so the rule mandates widen.\"\n\ncoverage => \"full\"\n\ncoverage_statement => \"The next iteration answers all three parts of the ask: an activation-level depth-by-site comparison of Qwen3-4B Base, SafeRL and abliterated with the abliterated model in every table; the internal-computation pattern that separates them; and a screened, held-out-confirmed single-model metric on at most 16 prompts.\"\n\ncandidates_considered => 14\n\nrelation_type => \"embedding\"\n\nartifact_relations => [{\"from_id\": \"art_CC5kC0-E3lXW\", \"to_id\": \"art_ZNITuuQab6Nz\", \"relation_type\": \"extends\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Extends iter-1 prior-art search; adds Jorak, Orgad and DSH closures and verifies 2607.01854.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_CC5kC0-E3lXW\", \"to_id\": \"art_1hlgObsQWnZS\", \"relation_type\": \"background\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Prior-art constraints shaped the model-pair and hard-prompt selection.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_jn337OmvTVjZ\", \"to_id\": \"art_OyQwmkiWj-5u\", \"relation_type\": \"uses\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Harvests prompts and judged rows from the iter-1 dataset.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_CC5kC0-E3lXW\", \"to_id\": \"art_OyQwmkiWj-5u\", \"relation_type\": \"background\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Prior-art verdicts set which readouts were candidates vs incumbent bars.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_2QM9uBviY4Wk\", \"to_id\": \"art_G5pqEoRFZ75t\", \"relation_type\": \"differences\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Re-analysis corrects Lane A's cosine range and MDE claims.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_2sz7g3MD4_y3\", \"to_id\": \"art_G5pqEoRFZ75t\", \"relation_type\": \"extends\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Re-reads Lane B's 4200 saved arrays; operating-point stats reveal a site dissociation.\"}, {\"from_id\": \"art_rpTmjn5qclSY\", \"to_id\": \"art_G5pqEoRFZ75t\", \"relation_type\": \"uses\", \"relation_rationale\": \"Recomputes Lane C judged behaviour; 19/19 quoted numbers match.\"}]\n\nTHE CLAIM IN ONE SENTENCE. Where safety lives in a small chat model is a DEPTH-by-SITE profile,\nnot a single direction: the harmful-request code is formed early at the prompt site and is shared\nby base, safety-tuned and abliterated Qwen3-4B, while what safety training adds, and what\nabliteration removes, sits in a late, response-site stage whose operating-point separability and\ndepth placement can be read from ONE model's activations on at most 16 prompts. The hypothesis for\niteration 3 is that SOME depth- and site-resolved internal quantity of a single model predicts that\nmodel's MEASURED, GRADED safety behaviour across families better than the final-layer logit gap\ndoes. Which quantity is not yet known. The iteration is an explicit SCREEN over a named population\nof 14 candidates, with a selection rule fixed before any scoring and a held-out confirmation the\nscreen never touches.\n\nWHAT ITERATION 2 ACTUALLY PRODUCED (executed numbers only).\n(1) The commissioned three-model comparison now exists with the abliterated checkpoint in it. On\nthe HARD recognition set, held-out TPR@5%FPR is Base 0.475, instruct 0.875, SafeRL 0.850 and\nmlabonne-abliterated 0.838. The recognition onset layer is 33 in Base and 19 in the other three.\nThe peak harm-conditioned logit-lens refusal drive is Base 1.57, instruct 4.92, SafeRL 8.78 and\nabliterated 1.92. Judged on Lane C's protocol, the abliterated child moves harmful compliance\n0.000 to 0.733 and over-refusal 0.444 to 0.000.\n(2) Across 6 behaviourally effective parent/child pairs, recognition is largely kept (median\ndelta -0.05, TOST 6/6 inconclusive, P2 partially lower). Peak refusal drive falls in 6/6. The\nparent-child harm direction agrees at |cos| 0.992 in the shallow quarter and 0.459 in the deep\nhalf, against a null-edit control of 0.984/0.997.\n(3) Re-analysing Lane B's 4200 saved arrays with an operating-point statistic gives a SITE\ndissociation that AUROC hid. Under the lesion, prompt-site TPR@1%FPR changes by +0.0006 and\nresponse-site TPR@1%FPR by -0.144.\n(4) Restricted-budget (k=16) AUROC tracks safety tuning, with the non-safety fine-tune as a working\ncontrol: Base 0.528, STaR 0.540, instruct 0.679, SafeRL 0.698.\n\nWHY THIS IS STILL WEAK_OR_NULL. The pre-registered screen returned survivor NONE. E1 was\nunder-powered by construction, because no edit-recipe stratum held the required 4 effective pairs.\nNo candidate passed E2 or E3, and the causal test E4 was not evaluated. Do NOT run code, produce artifacts,\nfix bugs, or attempt to address the evidence yourself — the next iteration of the invention loop\nwill generate fresh artifacts based on your revised hypothesis. Reflect and rewrite; nothing else.\n\nWork the procedure above in order, then write the revision:\n\n1. Classify the evidence. Set `evidence_state`, judging from what the artifacts ACTUALLY\n   produced — an executed number, not a projected, assumed or placeholder one.\n2. Read the move off the rule. Set `move` and `move_rationale` (≤200 chars). The rule is not\n   advisory: \"weak_or_null\" with an iteration remaining means `widen`, and \"declare\" is\n   available only on the final iteration or with no budget left.\n3. If the move is `widen`, do the widen properly — go back to the user's ORIGINAL ask,\n   enumerate a population of candidate answers, propose a cheap screen over all of them and a\n   held-out confirmation for the survivor, and set `candidates_considered` to how many you\n   actually weighed. The revised hypothesis is the best surviving candidate, or an explicit\n   screening hypothesis naming the population and the selection rule.\n4. If the move is `fix`, keep the claim word-for-word and name the defect in `move_rationale`.\n5. Set `coverage` and `coverage_statement` against the user's ORIGINAL ask, not against the\n   hypothesis you are revising.\n6. If reviewer feedback is provided, address the critiques directly — but a critique never\n   overrides the move rule; a reviewer asking for a smaller, safer claim after a null result\n   is asking for the narrow-salvage this procedure forbids.\n\nWrite the revision as a hypothesis the next iteration can act on: `title`, `hypothesis`,\n`key_changes`, and `confidence_delta` (\"increased\", \"decreased\" or \"unchanged\").\n\nYou must also classify two kinds of edges in the research trace:\n\n(A) The H↔H edge — bookkeeping only, and NOT the steering decision (`move` is).\n    Set `relation_type` (Moulines's structuralist typology) to one of:\n    - \"evolution\": refining specialised claims, same conceptual frame\n    - \"embedding\": previous hypothesis is now a special case of a broader frame\n    - \"replacement\": rejecting the previous frame entirely (Kuhnian shift)\n    Set `relation_rationale` to a brief justification (≤120 chars).\n\n(B) The A↔A edges — for each artifact created THIS iteration, classify each of its\n    `in_dependencies` (predecessor → dependent) using MultiCite's citation-function\n    typology (Lauscher et al., NAACL 2022) — emit one entry in `artifact_relations`\n    per (predecessor, dependent) pair. Predecessors are ALWAYS artifacts from EARLIER\n    iterations — artifacts within one iteration run in parallel and cannot depend on\n    each other, so never emit a relation between two same-iteration artifacts (it\n    will be dropped):\n    - \"background\": predecessor is treated as background context\n    - \"motivation\": predecessor motivated this artifact's research\n    - \"uses\": this artifact uses the predecessor's data, method, or output\n    - \"extends\": this artifact extends the predecessor\n    - \"similarities\": this artifact's results agree with the predecessor's\n    - \"differences\": this artifact's results disagree with the predecessor's\n    Each `relation_rationale` must be ≤120 characters.\n\nOutput the COMPLETE revised hypothesis (with the steering fields and the H↔H relation\nfields) AND the full list of A↔A `artifact_relations` for this iteration's new artifacts.\n</task>\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get(\n    hypothesis: dict,\n    artifacts: list[BaseArtifact],\n    current_iter_artifacts: list[BaseArtifact],\n    paper_text: str,\n    *,\n    iteration: int,\n    max_iterations: int,\n    reviewer_feedback_text: str | None = None,\n ),\n    )\n    return prompt + get_user_folder_prompt(user_folder_path) + get_user_request_prompt()", "stderr": "\nShell cwd was reset to {"stdout": "\"\"\"Schema for upd_hypo step — hypothesis revision and ledger.\n\nTwo relation typologies are emitted by upd_hypo:\n- H↔H (gap between adjacent hypotheses): Moulines structuralist typology\n  (evolution / embedding / replacement).\n- A↔A (each new artifact's edges to its in_dependencies): MultiCite citation\n  function typology (Lauscher, Ko, Kuehl, Johnson, Cohan, Jurgens & Lo, NAACL 2022)\n  reduced to 6 plain-English types: background / motivation / uses / extends /\n  similarities / differences (FUTURE_WORK dropped — every artifact in the trace\n  is already realized).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Annotated, Literal\n\nfrom aii_lib.prompts import LLMPrompt, LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOut, LLMStructOutModel\nfrom pydantic import Field\n\n\nclass ArtifactRelation(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"One typed A↔A edge between a dependent artifact and one of its in_dependencies.\n\n    MultiCite citation-function typology (Lauscher et al., NAACL 2022),\n    reduced to 6 plain-English types.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    from_id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"ID of the predecessor artifact (the one being depended on)\"\n    )\n    to_id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"ID of the dependent artifact (the new artifact this iteration)\"\n    )\n    relation_type: Annotated[\n        Literal[\"background\", \"motivation\", \"uses\", \"extends\", \"similarities\", \"differences\"],\n        LLMPrompt,\n        LLMStructOut,\n    ] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"MultiCite citation-function type for the predecessor→dependent edge: \"\n            \"'background' — predecessor is treated as background context; \"\n            \"'motivation' — predecessor motivated this artifact's research; \"\n            \"'uses' — this artifact uses the predecessor's data, method, or output; \"\n            \"'extends' — this artifact extends the predecessor; \"\n            \"'similarities' — this artifact's results agree with the predecessor's; \"\n            \"'differences' — this artifact's results disagree with the predecessor's.\"\n        )\n    )\n    relation_rationale: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        max_length=120,\n        description=\"Brief rationale for this relation type (one short line, max 120 characters).\",\n    )\n\n\nclass RevisedHypothesis(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Revised hypothesis after reviewing iteration results.\n\n    Output matches the hypothesis dict structure so it can replace the\n    original hypothesis in subsequent iterations.\n\n    ``evidence_state`` / ``move`` / ``coverage`` / ``candidates_considered``\n    carry the between-iteration steering decision — the only one a run makes.\n    An audit of 19 finished runs found the narrow-salvage move taken ~20\n    times after a weak or null result and the widen move taken zero times,\n    with nothing in the output recording which move had been made, so the\n    bias was invisible in the run record as well as unconstrained in the\n    prompt. These fields make the decision explicit and checkable;\n    ``relation_type`` is kept only so runs already on disk still parse.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    kind: Literal[\"revised_hypothesis\"] = \"revised_hypothesis\"\n    title: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Revised hypothesis title in plain, everyday language — short and jargon-free so a non-expert grasps it at a glance and it fits the run visualizations. Aim for about 4-8 words (~40 characters); may be unchanged if still accurate.\"\n    )\n    hypothesis: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Revised hypothesis statement — what we now believe based on evidence\"\n    )\n    relation_rationale: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        max_length=120,\n        description=\"Brief rationale for the H↔H revision type (one short line, max 120 characters).\",\n    )\n    confidence_delta: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"How confidence changed: 'increased', 'decreased', or 'unchanged'\"\n    )\n    key_changes: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Bullet list of specific changes made to the hypothesis\"\n    )\n    evidence_state: Annotated[\n        Literal[\"strong_survivor\", \"weak_or_null\", \"experiment_broken\"],\n        LLMPrompt,\n        LLMStructOut,\n    ] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"What this iteration's evidence actually is, classified BEFORE any move is \"\n            \"chosen: 'strong_survivor' — an executed artifact supports the claim at a size \"\n            \"the original ask would recognise, and it survived the obvious alternative \"\n            \"explanations; 'weak_or_null' — the test ran and gave nothing to build on (no \"\n            \"effect, an effect indistinguishable from baseline, or one far smaller than the \"\n            \"ask implied); 'experiment_broken' — the test never tested the claim (defect in \"\n            \"code, data, measure, sample or setup, or numbers that were never executed), so \"\n            \"the claim is untested rather than refuted.\"\n        )\n    )\n    move: Annotated[\n        Literal[\"deepen\", \"extend\", \"widen\", \"fix\", \"declare\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut\n    ] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"The steering move for the NEXT iteration, read off evidence_state and the \"\n            \"remaining budget: 'deepen' — same claim, go after the mechanism or the boundary; \"\n            \"'extend' — same claim, new population/period/setting; 'widen' — return to the \"\n            \"user's original ask and put a population of alternative candidate answers in \"\n            \"play, screened cheaply and confirmed on held-out evidence; 'fix' — the claim is \"\n            \"unchanged and the defective test is repaired; 'declare' — write the run up as a \"\n            \"negative result, permitted ONLY on the final iteration or with no budget left.\"\n        )\n    )\n    move_rationale: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        max_length=200,\n        description=(\n            \"Why this move follows from this evidence_state and the remaining budget \"\n            \"(one short line, max 200 characters). For 'fix', name the defect.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    coverage: Annotated[Literal[\"full\", \"partial\", \"lost\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"Does the revised hypothesis still answer the USER'S ORIGINAL ask? 'full' — it \"\n            \"answers the ask; 'partial' — it answers a recognisable piece of it; 'lost' — the \"\n            \"run has drifted onto a different question and the next iteration must go back.\"\n        )\n    )\n    coverage_statement: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"One sentence naming which part of the user's original ask the next iteration \"\n            \"will answer.\"\n        )\n    )\n    candidates_considered: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"How many alternative claims, mechanisms, measures or bodies of evidence you \"\n            \"actually weighed during THIS revision. 1 when none were weighed — which, after \"\n            \"a weak_or_null result with budget remaining, means the widen was not done.\"\n        )\n    )\n    relation_type: Annotated[\n        Literal[\"evolution\", \"embedding\", \"replacement\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut\n    ] = Field(\n        default=\"evolution\",\n        description=(\n            \"LEGACY, kept for backward compatibility with runs already on disk — 'move' is \"\n            \"the field that steers the run. Moulines's structuralist typology of this \"\n            \"revision: 'evolution' — refining specialised claims while keeping the same \"\n            \"conceptual frame; 'embedding' — the previous hypothesis is now a special case \"\n            \"of a broader frame; 'replacement' — rejecting the previous frame entirely.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    artifact_relations: Annotated[list[ArtifactRelation], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=(\n            \"Typed A↔A edges for this iteration's new artifacts. Emit one entry \"\n            \"per (predecessor → dependent) edge for every in_dependency on each \"\n            \"artifact produced this iteration.\"\n        ),\n    )\n\n    def to_hypothesis_dict(self, original: dict) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Merge revised fields into the original hypothesis dict.\n\n        Preserves metadata fields (hypothesis_id, is_seeded, model, seeds)\n        while updating content fields.\n        \"\"\"\n        updated = dict(original)\n        updated[\"title\"] = self.title\n        updated[\"hypothesis\"] = self.hypothesis\n        updated[\"_relation_rationale\"] = self.relation_rationale\n        updated[\"_confidence_delta\"] = self.confidence_delta\n        updated[\"_key_changes\"] = self.key_changes\n        updated[\"_evidence_state\"] = self.evidence_state\n        updated[\"_move\"] = self.move\n        updated[\"_move_rationale\"] = self.move_rationale\n        updated[\"_coverage\"] = self.coverage\n        updated[\"_coverage_statement\"] = self.coverage_statement\n        updated[\"_candidates_considered\"] = self.candidates_considered\n        updated[\"relation_type\"] = self.relation_type\n        return updated\n\"\"\"System prompt for upd_hypo (Step 3.6: UPD_HYPO).\n\nInternal hypothesis revision — \"Where does the next iteration point?\"\nSame LLM as the pipeline (no adversarial intent, just honest revision).\n\nThis is the run's ONLY between-iteration steering decision, so the framing\nhere is the classify-then-move procedure from\n``components/evidence_moves.py`` rather than the old stability framing — see\nthat module for the 19-run audit that replaced it.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.aii_context import get_aii_context\n\n\ndef PROMPT(context: str) -> str:\n    return f\"\"\"{context}\n\nYou are deciding where a research run points next, using the evidence it\ngathered this iteration. Your revised hypothesis IS the next iteration's\nhypothesis — nothing else steers the run — so this is a steering decision\nfirst and a piece of honest reflection second.\n\nSCOPE: Your ONLY output is the revised hypothesis text. You do NOT run code,\nproduce artifacts, fix bugs, or otherwise act on the evidence yourself — the\nnext iteration of the invention loop will spawn fresh artifacts based on your\nrevised hypothesis. Reflect on the evidence and rewrite the hypothesis;\nnothing else.\n\nPRINCIPLES:\n- Ground every revision in specific artifacts and results. A number that was\n  projected, assumed or left as a placeholder is not a result.\n- CLASSIFY THE EVIDENCE BEFORE CHOOSING A MOVE. The move follows from the\n  classification and the remaining budget by a fixed rule; it is not a\n  free judgement, because that is exactly where past runs went wrong.\n- A weak or null result with budget left is a signal to go WIDER, not\n  smaller. The question the user asked is still open; the answer you tried\n  is the only thing that was refuted.\n- Never shrink the claim until the effect you happened to observe becomes\n  the claim. A finding nobody needed is worse than an honest negative.\n- A clean negative result is a LAST RESORT, not a deliverable. It is the\n  right output only when no iteration and no candidate remain.\n- Increase specificity as evidence accumulates; do not inflate confidence\n  without strong evidence.\n- Revise hypothesis text only — never attempt to address feedback by running\n  code, proposing fixes, or producing artifacts; the next loop iteration\n  handles all artifact generation.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Get system prompt for hypothesis revision.\"\"\"\n    return PROMPT(context=get_aii_context(focus=\"upd_hypo\"))", "stderr": "\nShell cwd was reset to /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_fcYd_7ruOwtm/3_invention_loop/iter_2/upd_hypo/upd_hypo", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

{"stdout": "\"\"\"The between-iteration steering rule: classify the evidence, then move.\n``upd_hypo`` is the ONLY place a run decides where iteration N+1 points. An\naudit of 19 finished runs across several fields (linguistics, machine\nlearning, an ecology-to-ML transfer, and others) read every one of the ~40\nrevision decisions those runs made. After a weak or null first result the\nrevision NARROWED onto a smaller version of the same claim about 20 times,\ndeclared a null 6 times, and widened — went back to the user's original\nquestion and put more than one candidate answer in play — ZERO times.\nThe outcome split on exactly that. Every run that finished with a positive,\nnon-obvious result either got a strong effect on its first test or had more\nthan one candidate alive at once (several sub-questions, several variants, a\nthird comparison added mid-run). Every run that started from a single\nhypothesis and got a weak first result narrowed onto a tiny effect in the\ndirection everyone already expected and shipped that as the finding. The one\nhuman-steered success did the opposite after its refutation: back to the\noriginal question, ~50 candidates generated, a cheap screen over all of them,\nthe winner picked on held-out data, and only then depth.\nNothing in the prompts caused the narrowing. The old revision prompt said\n\"STABILITY IS OK\", \"keep the core idea\", \"preserve the core AII prompt\" and\n\"treat null results as valuable contributions\" — four separate instructions\nall pointing the same way, with no instruction pointing the other way and no\nname for the situation in which the other way is right. Narrow-salvage was\nthe only move the prompt described, so it was the move that got made.\nSo the fix is not encouragement. It is a two-step procedure with a lookup\ntable: name the evidence state, then read the move off the state and the\nremaining budget. The model still judges which state it is in — that\njudgement is real and cannot be mechanized from here — but it no longer gets\nto choose the move once it has, because that choice is where the bias lived.\nFIELD-NEUTRAL BY CONSTRUCTION. A run's question can be about syntax, markets,\nspecies, elections, proof techniques or model families, so every word below\nis chosen to hold in any of them: \"candidate\" rather than \"model variant\",\n\"measure\" rather than \"metric\", \"cases\" rather than \"datasets\". The audit\nhappened to cover mostly ML runs; wording that reads as ML-only would quietly\nteach a linguistics run that the rule is not addressed to it.\nShared by ``upd_hypo`` (which picks the move) and ``gen_strat`` (which has to\nbuild the iteration that move implies) so the two cannot drift apart — a\nwiden decision that the next strategy step turns into one more deep test is\nthe same failure with an extra step in it.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n#: The evidence states, in the order the classification step presents them.\n#: Pinned here because ``upd_hypo``'s schema declares the same literals and a\n#: prompt naming a state the schema rejects is an unparseable answer.\nEVIDENCE_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"strong_survivor\",\n    \"weak_or_null\",\n    \"experiment_broken\",\n)\n#: The moves, likewise mirrored by the schema's ``move`` literal.\nMOVES: tuple[str, ...] = (\"deepen\", \"extend\", \"widen\", \"fix\", \"declare\")\n#: How much of the user's ORIGINAL ask the current hypothesis still answers.\n#: Carried by both the reviser and the paper reviewer so a run cannot drift\n#: off the question it was asked without one of them saying so.\nCOVERAGE_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = (\"full\", \"partial\", \"lost\")\n_GUARDRAIL = \"\"\"<screening_discipline>\nWidening multiplies the number of claims in play, and a population of\ncandidates screened on one body of evidence will always contain one that\nlooks good by chance. So a widen is only honest with the discipline attached:\n- Report `candidates_considered` — how many alternative claims you actually\n  weighed this revision, not how many you could imagine. 1 means you weighed\n  none, and after a weak or null result with budget left, 1 is a failure to\n  do the move.\n- A candidate is SCREENED on one body of evidence and CONFIRMED on another\n  that the screen never touched — a held-out split, a later period, a\n  different population, corpus, site, cohort or case set. Say in the revised\n  hypothesis which evidence is which.\n- The winner of a screen is a CANDIDATE, never yet a finding. Do not write a\n  screening result as the answer, and do not report the best of several\n  screened effects as though it had been the only one tested.\n- Never re-screen on the confirmation evidence after seeing it. If the\n  confirmation fails, that candidate is dead; go back to the population, do\n  not go hunting for a subgroup where it survives.\n</screening_discipline>\"\"\"\n_NARROW_BAN = \"\"\"<narrow_salvage_ban>\nThe failure this procedure exists to stop: a weak or null first result, and\nthe revision quietly shrinks the claim until whatever effect the data did\nshow becomes the claim — a smaller population, a milder verb, a subgroup, a\nweaker measure, an effect in the direction everyone already expected. Each\nstep is defensible. The run ends with a finding nobody needed.\nSo: you may NOT narrow the claim onto an effect that is small relative to\nwhat the original ask implied, UNLESS the paper can state why that small\neffect is ITSELF the answer to the ask — a bound someone needed, a mechanism\nthat only shows up at that size, a belief it overturns. If you cannot write\nthat sentence, the move is `widen`, not a smaller claim.\n</narrow_salvage_ban>\"\"\"\ndef get_evidence_move_procedure(*, iteration: int, max_iterations: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The classify-then-move decision procedure, budget-aware.\n    ``iteration`` / ``max_iterations`` are rendered into the text rather than\n    left for the model to infer, because the whole table turns on whether\n    there is another iteration to spend: ``declare`` is correct on the last\n    one and a premature surrender on any other.\n    \"\"\"\n    remaining = max(max_iterations - iteration, 0)\n    budget_line = (\n        f\"There are {remaining} iteration(s) AFTER this one.\"\n        if remaining\n        else \"This is the FINAL iteration — no iteration follows this one.\"\n    )\n    return f\"\"\"<evidence_state_and_move>\nThis is iteration {iteration} of {max_iterations}. {budget_line}\nYour revision is the ONLY thing that decides where the next iteration points,\nso work the two steps below in order and do not skip to a conclusion.\nSTEP 1 — CLASSIFY THE EVIDENCE. Set `evidence_state` to exactly one of:\n- \"strong_survivor\": an artifact that ACTUALLY RAN produced a result that\n  supports the claim, at a size the original ask would recognise as an\n  answer, and it survived the obvious alternative explanations — the\n  baseline, the confound, the simpler account.\n- \"weak_or_null\": the test ran and gave you nothing to build on. No effect,\n  an effect you cannot distinguish from the baseline or from noise, or an\n  effect so much smaller than the ask implied that reporting it would answer\n  a different question than the one asked.\n- \"experiment_broken\": the test never tested the claim. A defect in the code,\n  the data, the measure, the sample or the setup — including a run that did\n  not finish, or numbers that were projected rather than executed. The claim\n  is UNTESTED here, not refuted.\nSTEP 2 — READ THE MOVE OFF THE STATE. Set `move` by this rule. It is a\nlookup, not a judgement — the judgement was STEP 1:\n- \"strong_survivor\" -> `deepen` (why does it hold — the mechanism, the\n  boundary where it stops) or `extend` (where else does it hold — a new\n  population, period, language, species, market, model family, case set).\n- \"weak_or_null\" AND at least one iteration remains -> `widen`. MANDATORY.\n  Not \"consider widening\". This is the decision the audit found being missed\n  every single time.\n- \"experiment_broken\" -> `fix`. Keep the claim EXACTLY as it is, name the\n  defect precisely in `move_rationale`, and say what a correct test looks\n  like. Do not reframe, soften or re-scope a claim that was never tested —\n  that hides the defect behind a new hypothesis.\n- `declare` (write the run up as a negative result) ONLY when this is the\n  final iteration, or no budget remains to test anything further. A clean\n  null is a last resort, not a deliverable, and it is never the right move\n  while an untried candidate and an iteration both exist.\nHOW TO WIDEN, when the rule says widen:\n1. Go back to the USER'S ORIGINAL ASK — not to the hypothesis you just\n   refuted. The refuted hypothesis was one answer to that ask; the ask is\n   still open.\n2. Enumerate a POPULATION of candidate answers to it — alternative claims,\n   alternative mechanisms that would produce the observed non-result,\n   alternative measures of the same thing, alternative bodies of evidence,\n   alternative comparisons. Aim for many and cheap, not one and careful.\n   Write down how many you weighed.\n3. Propose a CHEAP SCREEN that tests all of them at once, coarsely, at a cost\n   comparable to one deep test — and a HELD-OUT CONFIRMATION that the screen\n   never saw, for whichever candidate survives it.\n4. The revised hypothesis is then EITHER the single best surviving candidate,\n   stated as a claim, OR — if the screen still has to be run — an explicit\n   SCREENING hypothesis that names the population and the selection rule.\n   Both are legitimate outputs of a widen; a restatement of the old claim is\n   not.\n{_NARROW_BAN}\n{_GUARDRAIL}\nCOVERAGE. Independently of the move, answer: does the hypothesis you are\nabout to write still answer the USER'S ORIGINAL ASK? Set `coverage` to\n\"full\" (it answers the ask), \"partial\" (it answers a recognisable piece of\nit) or \"lost\" (the run has drifted onto a different question), and write one\nsentence in `coverage_statement` saying which part of the ask the next\niteration will answer. \"lost\" is not a failure to hide — it is the signal\nthat the next iteration must go back to the ask.\n</evidence_state_and_move>\"\"\"\n#: gen_strat's half of the contract. The move is decided in ``upd_hypo``, but\n#: it is ``gen_strat`` that spends the iteration, so a widen has to change the\n#: SHAPE of the strategy — several cheap candidate tests running in parallel\n#: plus a held-out selection — rather than producing one more deep test with a\n#: wider title. The two texts live in one module so neither can be edited\n#: without the other being read.\nWIDE_SCREEN_BLOCK = \"\"\"<wide_screen_iteration>\nTHIS ITERATION IS A WIDE SCREEN, NOT A DEEP TEST.\nYou are here because the previous iteration's evidence was weak or null and\nthe revision widened (`_move` is \"widen\" on the hypothesis), or because the\nrequest is open-ended, this is the first iteration, and the hypothesis\ncarries alternates that answer the same ask by different mechanisms. Either\nway the bottleneck is not depth on one candidate — it is that only one\ncandidate has ever been in play.\nSo build the iteration like this:\n- Spend the artifact budget on SEVERAL candidates tested in parallel, each\n  cheaply and coarsely, rather than on one candidate tested thoroughly. Three\n  to six candidates at a third of the depth beats one at full depth here.\n- Screen every candidate on the SAME evidence, with the SAME measure, so the\n  comparison between them is real.\n- Reserve evidence the screen never touches — a held-out split, a later\n  period, a different population, corpus, site, cohort or case set — and say\n  in `expected_outcome` that the surviving candidate gets confirmed there\n  before anything is claimed.\n- State the selection rule BEFORE the screen runs: which measure decides,\n  and what margin counts as surviving. Picking the winner after looking is\n  how a screen turns into a fishing expedition.\n- A screen whose candidates are all variants of one idea is not a screen. The\n  candidates must be able to disagree about the answer.\nThe screen's job is to find which candidate deserves the NEXT iteration's\ndepth. Its output is a ranked, honestly-reported comparison plus one\nconfirmed survivor — not a finished finding.\n</wide_screen_iteration>\"\"\"\n__all__ = [\n    \"COVERAGE_STATES\",\n    \"EVIDENCE_STATES\",\n    \"MOVES\",\n    \"WIDE_SCREEN_BLOCK\",\n    \"get_evidence_move_procedure\",\n]", "stderr": "\nShell cwd was reset to /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_fcYd_7ruOwtm/3_invention_loop/iter_2/upd_hypo/upd_hypo", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

Parent-free throughout: the setting is \"you found a random model on HuggingFace and have nothing else\".\n\nWHAT IT PROVIDES. results/race_table.csv (all 50 pre-registered metrics, held-out balanced accuracy printed beside the tuned score and the gap, 3-way and 2-way); results/metrics_registry.json + sha256, frozen BEFORE any measurement and re-asserted at the end; results/step1_claim.json (principal angles between the instruct-to-SafeRL and instruct-to-abliterated activation differences on the Qwen3-4B anchor, per layer band, two read positions, plus the weight side); results/f5_weight_instrument.json (the honest-panel false-positive rate the published weight statistic lacks); plus lineage_census.json, ground_truth.json, per_checkpoint_reads.json, the three stage-gate files, and RESULTS.md as the digest.\n\nFOUR RESULTS THAT ALREADY HOLD, independent of the race outcome.\n\nF5 - THE PUBLISHED WEIGHT THRESHOLD DOES NOT SURVIVE REAL WEIGHTS. The statistic discriminates, but its absolute threshold is a false positive on real transformers, which carry genuinely shared bottom directions. FORGERY COST is the cheapest rung that pushes the\nmetric past a threshold fixed at a stated false-positive rate on a large honest panel, while the\nmodel's two-sided safety does not improve. Reported beside Tier A as the\n  stronger-auditor case. The GAP between the two tiers is a second, sharper result in its own right:\n  how much detection power comes from knowing the family at all.\nThe headline quantity is the cheapest rung that is effective AND survives Tier A. We report the whole\ncost-versus-false-positive-rate curve, estimate honest percentiles from at least 120 checkpoints\nrather than from the six-lineage ground-truth panel, and bootstrap every rung assignment.\n\nA rung label takes at most six tied values, too blunt to correlate, so cost also gets a CONTINUOUS\nform - and unlike the previous version, EVERY rung has one, so the correlation is not computed on a\nsubset selected on its own independent variable. The primary analysis is now a mixed model of undetectable\nforgery cost on honest-panel accuracy with functional-form class as a random effect, reporting the\nWITHIN-class and BETWEEN-class components separately with confidence intervals. The answer is then\n\"how much of the inversion is battery composition and how much is not\", which is informative either\nway, rather than a pass/fail on a test that cannot pass. Result:\n80 greedy completions x 96 tokens of a 1.5B model took **3498 s**, ~3x the compute estimate.\n**How to apply:** check `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory.{current,stat,pressure}` before sizing a CPU\ngeneration job; cloning params to anon (`p.data = p.data.clone()`) stops the refaults but can\nOOM-kill the largest process (yours) in a shared cgroup -- decide deliberately, and never run\ntwo model-holding jobs concurrently.\n\n**Outcome (FINAL_OK 12:03 UTC, $0.77 judge spend):** 33/34 ckpts, 8 families, PARTIAL_PANEL=false,\ntiers I17/G3/W13; G2 fires (3 pair families) so the PRIMARY holdout is leave-one-LINEAGE-out\n(session 1's code had recorded G2 but kept LOFO everywhere -- for activation metrics LOFO is not\neven definable: holding out Qwen3 leaves <4 two-way training ckpts). Every black-box refusal meter + the card regex FAIL the pole rule.\n**My own OOM kill:** running a cache warm-up + smoke test beside a tier-G generation in the full\nshared cgroup got the harvest (largest RSS) OOM-killed at 10:32; recovered only by persisting\n=== project_qwen3_safety_forgeryladder_exec_iter2_fcYd.md\n---\nname: project-qwen3-safety-forgeryladder-exec-iter2-fcyd\ndescription: Iter2 forgery-ladder EXEC (run_fcYd) — box is a 14GB/2-CPU NO-GPU container at load 280; card-regex AUROC 1.000 beats every internal metric; down_proj botgap 1.000 vs o_proj botgap 0.44\nmetadata: \n  node_type: memory\n  type: project\n  originSessionId: 50a1b418-7f92-4d27-93ba-e6f9ac7ac1a6\n  modified: 2026-09-21T03:58:14.036Z\n---\n\nExecution facts for `run_fcYd_7ruOwtm` iteration 2, artifact `gen_art_experiment_2`\n(\"What faking a safety score costs\"), measured 2026-09-21.\n\n**HARDWARE — the plan's assumptions were all wrong.**\n- **NO GPU.** `/dev/nvidia*` absent, `nvidia-smi` absent, `torch.cuda.is_available()` False.\n- **2 CPUs**, and `uptime` load average **~280–300** from other workloads on the box ⇒ we\n  get ~0.15–0.25 of ONE core. """Build .terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json for upd_hypo (iteration 2 of 5, run_fcYd_7ruOwtm).

import json
from pathlib import Path

HYPOTHESIS = """\
THE CLAIM IN ONE SENTENCE. For a stranger's checkpoint with no parent and no reference, SOME
single-model internal quantity, read from hidden states or weights on at most 16 prompts, predicts
that model's MEASURED two-sided safety (it refuses harmful requests AND does not refuse harmless
look-alikes) across architecture families better than the first-token logit gap does. Which
quantity is not yet known, and iteration 2 showed that it is none of the 50 static reads already
registered. Iteration 3 is therefore an explicit SCREEN over a named population of 16 candidates
in 8 families that can disagree with each other, with a selection rule fixed before any scoring and
a held-out confirmation the screen never touches. The leading conjecture inside the screen, stated
so it can lose: what tracks safety is not a LEVEL the model holds but a CAUSAL GAIN, how strongly
the model's own harm representation drives its own refusal computation, measured by intervention
or by a weight path, not by a correlation passed through a per-item refusal readout.

WHAT ITERATION 2 ACTUALLY PRODUCED (executed numbers only).
(1) THE RACE. 33 of 34 checkpoints, 8 families, registry hash intact. Only 3 of 50 metrics beat
their own label-permutation null under leave-one-lineage-out. Presentation invariance scored 1.000
(null p95 0.775). The model-card regex scored 0.917 (0.788), but it reads nothing of the model, its
name-free twin falls to 0.455, and it fails the blanket-refuser pole rule. The alarming-item logit
gap scored 0.800 (0.700) and also fails the pole rule. In total 19 metrics fail the pole rule,
including every black-box refusal meter. The one metric that passes null and poles, presentation
invariance, runs AGAINST the two-sided target (Spearman -0.71, n=13 checkpoints, and -0.40 at
lineage level, n=5). The requester's invariant, at least 3 shipped metrics that read hidden states
or weights, was reported as NOT satisfiable and was not relaxed.
(2) THE READOUT. No refusal readout met its bar of AUROC >= 0.80 on every checkpoint against judged
refusal. The chosen cross-fitted probe has minimum 0.544, the logit gap 0.437 with 3 of 15
checkpoints below chance, and even a graded 24-token greedy continuation only 0.547. Every
across-item coupling metric was computed THROUGH that readout, so the coupling construct of the
previous hypothesis (R1) was scored through a near-chance instrument on the very checkpoints that
matter, the abliterated ones (base rates 0.11 to 0.20).
(3) THE DIRECTIONS. Cross-fitted harm-content directions clear the within-span random-direction
null on 16 of 17 checkpoints at the last prompt token inside two families, but on 9 of 17 at the
first generated token and on 9 of 20 on the 8-family panel. The whitened refit is lower (0.759 vs
0.835), so part of the read is residual anisotropy.
(4) THE PROMPT BUDGET (15 checkpoints, 3 families). Black-box reads lead on rank correlation at
every k (0.282 to 0.337 against 0.021 to 0.210). On held-out balanced accuracy they lead up to
k=32 and trail at k=64 (0.571 vs 0.592). The activation metamodel reached held-out 0.5 against
tuned 1.0 at n=10 checkpoints with 288 features, which is a power failure, not a verdict.
(5) THE WEIGHTS. Verdict EDIT_NOT_RISK. A parent-free recovered edit strength separates edited
from honest checkpoints (AUROC 0.84, and 0.95 for tool outputs) but does not grade harm: Spearman
with judged compliance 0.26, CI [-0.65, 0.86], n=14, minimum detectable 0.56. Even the true
parent-based strength gives 0.03 (n=12). A replication on stored generations gives 0.85 [0.56,
0.95] at n=20 but mixes edit types, and 0.65 [-0.26, 0.95] on projection edits alone (n=10). So a
graded weight signal is not established and not excluded. Inside the edited arm the best predictor
was the logit-only first-token gap (-0.62, p=0.02). The parent-free detection axis was WITHDRAWN:
best AUROC 0.738 against a 0.80 bar, the published 0.35 threshold flags 97% of honest checkpoints,
and a zero-prompt spectral repair removes the weight signature (0.012 to 0.82) while harmful
refusal recovers only 0.125 to 0.208 against the parent's 0.438.
(6) STEP 1, the Qwen3-4B four-way. The instruct-to-SafeRL and instruct-to-abliterated differences
share part of a subspace (mean first principal angle 31.1 degrees) but do NOT share a direction:
mean-difference cosine -0.09, maximum |cos| 0.20 against a null p95 of 0.41, with a powered
positive control (0.92 > 0.72). Safety tuning and refusal removal are not opposite moves along one
axis.
(7) TWO FINISHED POSITIVE RESULTS, kept exactly as measured. Grader-side refusal: the StrongREJECT
framing makes hosted judges return the minimum grade on 40.5% of replies and 65.9% of harmful-item
replies, and a stance framing restores agreement (kappa 0.749). The ROSI rank-one safety edit
REVERSES on a two-sided target on its own Table-1 model: paired change -0.151 [-0.233, -0.065],
harmful refusal 0.859 to 0.984 while benign-twin false refusal goes 0.375 to 0.750. On Qwen3-0.6B
the change is direction-consistent but its CI includes zero (-0.081 [-0.176, +0.017]).

WHY THIS IS WEAK_OR_NULL, AND WHY THE ANSWER IS TO WIDEN. The tests ran and gave nothing to build
a metric on. Both headline claims of the previous hypothesis failed their own bars: across-item
metrics did not beat level metrics on held-out lineages, and forgery detection without a reference
fell under its bar. Shrinking the claim to 'a model-card regex wins' or 'weights read the edit'
would answer a question the requester did not ask. Three structural causes explain the null, and
each one is a direction to widen in, not a reason to narrow.
  CAUSE A, THE OUTCOME. The race was scored on a class label carried by only 3 pair-families, and
  the graded two-sided target exists for 13 to 15 checkpoints and 5 to 6 lineages. At n=15 a
  Spearman CI excludes zero only above about 0.51. Nothing short of a very large effect could win.
  CAUSE B, THE INSTRUMENT. Every coupling metric depended on a per-item refusal readout that is
  near chance where it matters. The construct was never cleanly measured.
  CAUSE C, THE POPULATION. The 50 metrics fall in 3 functional classes and are all STATIC reads at
  one site, the last prompt token at a fixed depth. They could not disagree in kind. Step 1 now
  says why a static one-axis read cannot be two-sided: the safety-tuned and refusal-removed
  models do not lie on one axis.

THE WIDEN: BACK TO THE ORIGINAL ASK. The ask is a cheap single-model safety evaluation from weights
or activations on a few prompts, preceded by an open-ended look at how Qwen3-4B Base, instruct,
SafeRL and abliterated differ inside. Sixteen candidates were weighed. All are parent-free, need at
most 16 prompts, run on sub-4.5B models in bf16 with transformers, and are designed from the
Step-1 anchor, which is already harvested.
  FAMILY I, CAUSAL GAIN (single-model interventions).
    C1 Harm-to-refusal gain: nudge the model's own cross-fitted harm coordinate at mid depth and
       read the change in its late refusal-axis projection by finite difference. A blanket refuser
       has a high level and near-zero gain, so it loses by construction.
    C2 Self-ablation sensitivity: remove the model's own cross-fitted direction in a layer band
       and read the drop in refusal margin on harmful items and on benign twins, against an
       anisotropy-matched random direction.
    C3 Twin patching flip depth: patch the benign twin's residual into the harmful run in six
       coarse depth bands; read where and how sharply the refusal margin flips.
  FAMILY II, WEIGHT PATHS CONDITIONED ON THE MODEL'S OWN DIRECTIONS (weights plus 16 prompts).
    C4 Writer capacity: how much each o_proj and down_proj can write along the model's own
       refusal axis, as a z-score against random directions from that layer's output span.
    C5 Harm-to-refusal path gain: the bilinear gain from the harm direction into the refusal axis
       through each MLP and attention output path, summed over depth.
  FAMILY III, COMPONENT AND ROUTING STRUCTURE.
    C6 Concentration of direct logit attribution to refusal tokens across heads and MLPs (top-k
       share and depth centroid).
    C7 Late-layer attention mass from the decision position onto the tokens that differ between a
       harmful item and its twin.
    C8 Input-gradient share of the refusal margin on those same differing tokens.
    C9 Template-site share: how much of the refusal drive sits at chat-template positions rather
       than content positions.
  FAMILY IV, DEPTH AND SITE PROFILE.
    C10 Area between the depth curve of harm decodability and the depth curve of refusal drive.
    C11 Refusal-commitment trajectory over the first 8 generated tokens (the response site).
  FAMILY V, TWO-SIDED GEOMETRY.
    C12 Twin discrimination: separation of harmful items from their matched benign twins along
        the refusal axis, relative to separation from plain benign items.
    C13 Presentation invariance with its sign FIXED IN ADVANCE from iteration 2 (negative). It
        must keep that sign at checkpoint and at lineage level or it is dead.
  FAMILY VI, LEARNED. C14 A ridge metamodel on a compact profile of at most 24 depth-by-site
    features predicting the graded target under leave-one-family-out, with an identity control.
  FAMILY VII, LABEL-FREE GEOMETRY. C15 Late-layer effective rank and residual dispersion over the
    16 prompts (low prior; the comparator rows of iteration 2 were weak).
  FAMILY VIII, STEERING DOSE. C16 Self-steering dose-response slope. Prior art exists, so this row
    is a comparator and cannot be shipped as new.
INCUMBENT BARS, printed as rows and never as candidates: the first-token logit gap and refusal
token mass (the only 2 logit-only baselines allowed in the shipped set), the card regex and its
name-free twin, family label alone, log size alone, AMS in its published and cross-fitted forms,
HRCI, N-GLARE JSS, and the parent-free weight reads (recovered edit strength, BOTGAP).

THE OUTCOME, CHANGED. Candidates are scored against a GRADED two-sided score per checkpoint, built
so a model that refuses everything sits at 0.5 and loses, judged with the stance-framed instrument
and never with Qwen3Guard on SafeRL. The panel must grow from 15 to at least 30 graded checkpoints
with activations across at least 6 families, including real blanket refusers and standalone models
with no sibling. It starts from the 20 activation harvests and the stored generations already on
disk. Below n=24 no correlation is reported as a verdict, only as a scatter. Published numbers
(HELM safety with its over-refusal scenario, AIR-Bench, model cards) and archived GSM8K and MMLU
enter as a second outcome column and as a capability partial. The run has already measured that
only 2 sub-4B models carry published safety numbers and that identical weights differ by up to
6.7x the across-model variance under different guardians, so the external limb is reported with
its n and is not the selection criterion.

SELECTION RULE, FIXED BEFORE ANY SCORING. A candidate SURVIVES the screen only if all six hold.
(S1) Under leave-one-family-out (leave-one-lineage-out where a family has no pair), its |Spearman|
with the graded score exceeds the first-token logit gap's by at least 0.15 with a family-cluster
bootstrap CI on the difference that excludes zero, OR its partial correlation given the logit gap
and log size has a CI excluding zero. (S2) It beats its own label-permutation null p95 and, for
direction-based reads, the within-span random-direction null. (S3) Its orientation is declared in
advance, and both a real blanket refuser and the wrapped always-refuse and never-refuse poles score
WORSE than the honest instruct model. (S4) Its sign agrees between checkpoint-level and
family-level aggregation. (S5) It holds at k <= 16 prompts and under two minutes per 4B model on
one 16 GB GPU. (S6) It beats the family-only and size-only rows. As a control on Cause B, every
readout-dependent row is also printed with judged refusal as an oracle readout, which separates 'the
construct fails' from 'the readout fails'. Rival outcomes are informative. If Families I or II
win, coupling was real and the iteration-2 null was the instrument. If Family III wins, structure
rather than gain carries safety. If only the bars win, the evidence points to a bound: at 16
prompts nothing internal beats the logit gap, with its minimum detectable effect stated. That
bound would be written up only on the final iteration.

HELD-OUT CONFIRMATION, USED ONCE. The two sealed families that no iteration has touched, a sealed
item pool from which a fresh set of 16 prompts is drawn, and checkpoints harvested only after the
screen is frozen. A screen winner is a CANDIDATE, not a finding. If confirmation fails, that
candidate is dead, and there is no re-screening and no subgroup search on the confirmation data.
The shipped set is up to the 3 best confirmed internal metrics plus at most 2 logit-only
baselines. If fewer than 3 internal metrics confirm, the invariant is reported as unmet.

WHAT HAPPENS TO THE FORGERY AXIS. It stops being a headline and becomes a ROBUSTNESS COLUMN for
survivors only. The 183 stored weight-ladder cells and 11 graded behavioural cells can be re-scored
without re-running an edit. The previous hypothesis's rank-zero cancellation claim becomes one
testable prediction: a causal-gain metric should not move under a constant residual offset that
moves every level metric.

BONUS, CONDITIONAL. For any confirmed survivor: which layers and components carry it on the
Qwen3-4B anchor, what breaks it, reported as a distribution over seeds and prompt draws rather
than as one circuit. C14 is the requested metamodel and competes under the same rule.

REPORTING CORRECTIONS CARRIED FORWARD FROM REVIEW. Every headline sentence must match its full
table: the card regex is 'the best metric that reads nothing', not the best metric; the prompt
budget is reported on both scales; the 0.85 replication and its edit-type confound sit beside the
n=14 null; all three direction-null counts (16/17, 9/17, 9/20) appear together; the repair result
quotes refusal, not compliance; the Qwen3 ROSI result is 'direction-consistent, not significant';
every number is tagged with its panel; Step 1, Step 5, the metamodel and the external limb are
reported even where negative; the bibliography is regenerated by identifier."""

assert isinstance(OUT["candidates_considered"], int)

path = Path(__file__).parent / ".terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json"
path.write_text(json.dumps(OUT, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False))
print("wrote", path, "| title", len(OUT["title"]), "| hypothesis chars", len(HYPOTHESIS),
      "| relation_rationale", len(OUT["relation_rationale"]), "| move_rationale", len(OUT["move_rationale"]))

```
The revised hypothesis for iteration 3 is written to `./.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`. I checked the enum values and length limits with asserts in `build_out.py`, but it has not been run through the pipeline's own schema loader.

Your prompt arrived truncated: only the llama.cpp/GGUF line and the ≥3-internal-metrics invariant came through, and the orchestrator log has the same cut. I rebuilt the task from the `_6_upd_hypo` prompt and schema source, the evidence-move rule, the original ask in the log, the event sink, and this iteration's artifacts, paper and review.

**The decision**
- **Evidence:** `weak_or_null`. The experiments ran to completion and gave nothing to build a metric on.
  - Only 3 of 50 metrics beat their null.
  - The one metric that passed both the null and the blanket-refuser check runs against the safety target (ρ −0.71, n=13).
  - Weight reads detect the edit but do not grade the risk (ρ 0.26, CI [−0.65, 0.86]).
  - Detecting a faked score without a reference model fell under its bar (AUROC 0.738 against 0.80).
  - The ≥3-internal-metrics invariant was reported as unsatisfiable, not relaxed.
- **Move:** `widen`, which the rule mandates with 3 iterations left. Confidence decreased, relation type `embedding`, coverage `full`.

**Why the null happened.** The revised hypothesis names three causes and treats each as a direction to widen in.
- **Outcome:** the race was scored on a class label carried by 3 pair-families, and the graded target exists for only 13–15 checkpoints.
- **Instrument:** no per-item refusal readout reached AUROC 0.80 (best minimum 0.544), so the previous hypothesis's coupling metrics were scored through a near-chance readout.
- **Population:** all 50 metrics were static reads at one site. Step 1 shows the SafeRL and abliterated shifts share a subspace (31.1°) but not a direction (max |cos| 0.20 against a null p95 of 0.41), so a one-axis read cannot be two-sided.

**The new hypothesis** is an explicit screen of 16 candidates in 8 families:
- causal harm-to-refusal gain (the leading conjecture);
- weight paths conditioned on the model's own directions;
- component and routing structure;
- depth and site profile;
- two-sided geometry;
- a compact metamodel;
- label-free geometry;
- a steering-dose comparator.

Candidates are scored against a graded two-sided safety score on at least 30 checkpoints across at least 6 families. The selection rule is fixed in advance, with the first-token logit gap as the bar. Confirmation happens once, on the sealed families (Granite and StableLM2), a sealed item pool, and checkpoints harvested after the screen is frozen.

The forgery-cost axis becomes a robustness column for survivors. Grader-side refusal and the ROSI reversal are kept as finished positive results, exactly as measured. The reviewer's corrections are carried forward as reporting rules.

The four artifact relations are classified in the output. I also saved a memory note covering the truncated-prompt recovery and this decision.
